I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble recognizing familiar people or objects, like not knowing what a common item is or who someone is that you know well?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I know people I know, and I recognize regular things. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble recognizing familiar people or objects, like not knowing what a common item is or who someone is that you know well?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I know people I know, and I recognize regular things. Nothing like that’s stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you used to care about, or not caring as much about your usual activities?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still care about the usual things… maybe I don’t get around to them as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble finding the right words when you're speaking or understanding what others are saying?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might pause sometimes like anyone does, but I haven’t had trouble understanding people or finding words in any big way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble starting tasks or keeping them going once you’ve begun, like starting a project but then not being able to follow through with the steps needed to finish it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I can start something, but keeping with it is the hard part. Like I’ll be at the bridge table, or in one of those Fix-It Friends planning meetings, trying to listen and stay with it, and then after a bit my mind just… drifts. Then I realize I missed a good chunk of what was said.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had any trouble keeping track of multiple things at once, like following a conversation while also doing something else, or managing more than one task at a time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. If I’m trying to follow what people are saying and there’s something else going on, I lose the thread pretty quick. Even just sitting at bridge or one of those Fix-It Friends meetings, I’ll mean to pay attention, and then my mind kind of slips off. Next thing I know, I’ve missed half of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble organizing your thoughts or planning out steps to complete a task, like figuring out how to approach a repair job or structure your day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed a big problem with planning steps like that. I mean, I can usually figure out what needs doing… I may not do it in any fancy order, but I get the general idea.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself less motivated to do things you used to do, even if you know you should, like keeping up with repairs or staying involved with your friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say less motivated exactly. I still mean to do those things. Sometimes I just don’t get around to it, but I haven’t really thought of it as losing interest or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself having trouble getting started on things, even when you know they need to be done, like chores around the house or calling someone back?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I put things off, but I don’t know if I’d call it trouble getting started. It’s just… things wait a bit, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s been happening enough that I figured I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these memory problems, and have they been getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: It’s been… maybe a little over a year. About a year and two months, I’d say. It got to be more of a problem after my heart scare, when I had to cut back on walking and doing projects. That stuff used to keep me steadier, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your thinking, behavior, or mood along with these memory problems, like feeling confused, getting lost in familiar places, or being more irritable or anxious than usual?\n\nPatient: Not really sure. I mean, I don’t think I’m getting lost or anything like that. Mood… I suppose I get frustrated sometimes, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble planning or following through with tasks that used to be routine for you, like managing bills or organizing a project?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. Mostly it’s things I should be able to keep straight, like follow-up appointments for my heart, calling the clinic back, or going to do a repair and realizing I didn’t bring the right tools. Stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble solving problems or making decisions that you didn’t used to have?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not that I’ve really noticed. Decisions maybe take me a bit longer sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m having trouble solving problems exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble judging distances, seeing things in 3D, or noticing objects on one side of your visual field?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble recognizing familiar people or objects, like not knowing what a common item is or who someone is that you know well?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I know people I know, and I recognize regular things. I still care about the usual things… maybe I don’t get around to them as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble finding the right words when you're speaking or understanding what others are saying?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might pause sometimes like anyone does, but I haven’t had trouble understanding people or finding words in any big way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble starting tasks or keeping them going once you’ve begun, like starting a project but then not being able to follow through with the steps needed to finish it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I don’t think I’m getting lost or anything like that. Mood… I suppose I get frustrated sometimes, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble planning or following through with tasks that used to be routine for you, like managing bills or organizing a project?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. Mostly it’s things I should be able to keep straight, like follow-up appointments for my heart, calling the clinic back, or going to do a repair and realizing I didn’t bring the right tools. Stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble solving problems or making decisions that you didn’t used to have?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not that I’ve really noticed. Decisions maybe take me a bit longer sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m having trouble solving problems exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble judging distances, seeing things in 3D, or noticing objects on one side of your visual field?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble recognizing familiar people or objects, like not knowing what a common item is or who someone is that you know well?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I know people I know, and I recognize regular things. Nothing like that’s stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you used to care about, or not caring as much about your usual activities?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still care about the usual things… maybe I don’t get around to them as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble finding the right words when you're speaking or understanding what others are saying?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might pause sometimes like anyone does, but I haven’t had trouble understanding people or finding words in any big way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble starting tasks or keeping them going once you’ve begun, like starting a project but then not being able to follow through with the steps needed to finish it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I can start something, but keeping with it is the hard part. Like I’ll be at the bridge table, or in one of those Fix-It Friends planning meetings, trying to listen and stay with it, and then after a bit my mind just… drifts. Then I realize I missed a good chunk of what was said.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had any trouble keeping track of multiple things at once, like following a conversation while also doing something else, or managing more than one task at a time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. If I’m trying to follow what people are saying and there’s something else going on, I lose the thread pretty quick. Even just sitting at bridge or one of those Fix-It Friends meetings, I’ll mean to pay attention, and then my mind kind of slips off. Next thing I know, I’ve missed half of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble organizing your thoughts or planning out steps to complete a task, like figuring out how to approach a repair job or structure your day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed a big problem with planning steps like that. I mean, I can usually figure out what needs doing… I may not do it in any fancy order, but I get the general idea.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself less motivated to do things you used to do, even if you know you should, like keeping up with repairs or staying involved with your friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say less motivated exactly. I still mean to do those things. Sometimes I just don’t get around to it, but I haven’t really thought of it as losing interest or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself having trouble getting started on things, even when you know they need to be done, like chores around the house or calling someone back?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I put things off, but I don’t know if I’d call it trouble getting started. It’s just… things wait a bit, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself less likely to take the initiative to start things, even things you used to do without being asked or reminded?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that. I don’t really think of it that way. If something needs doing, I’ll usually get to it… maybe not right away, but I haven’t noticed a big change like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you have more trouble starting things now than you used to, even when you know they need to be done?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s more than before. I mean, things take me a while sometimes, but I can’t say I’ve really noticed a big difference.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself needing others to prompt you to do things you would have taken care of on your own before, like making calls or getting to appointments?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess sometimes I do need a nudge now. Mostly with things like calling the clinic back or remembering those heart follow-up appointments. I’ve missed things I shouldn’t have, or I’ll show up not having what I needed for a repair job. It’s… frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you have more trouble organizing and carrying out tasks on your own now compared to before, even ones you used to handle without help?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe some, but I wouldn’t say it’s a whole different thing than before. I’ve always kind of done things my own way. Some stuff takes me longer to get sorted, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the problems you’re having with memory and staying focused are a noticeable change from how you were before, say, five or ten years ago?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s more noticeable now. I keep forgetting things I really shouldn’t — heart follow-ups, calling the clinic back, or I’ll show up for a repair job without the right tools. That’s the kind of thing that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your ability to start tasks and follow through with them has gotten worse compared to how you were even five years ago, like finishing a repair job or staying focused during a meeting?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, mostly with keeping my attention on things. Like I’ll be at the bridge table, or one of those Fix-It Friends planning meetings, and I mean to listen. Then after a bit my mind just… goes somewhere else, and I realize I missed a good chunk of what was said.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
